# Compendium of Essence
<note>original: "Sāra Saṃgraha"</note>

<margin>Ho · 1</margin> **Om. Salutations to the holy Lord Ganesha.** <note>Ganesha is traditionally invoked at the beginning of Hindu texts to remove obstacles.</note> Having bowed to the Lord of Obstacles [Ganesha], the Goddess Saraswati <note>the goddess of knowledge and the arts</note>, and the Guru, this **Compendium of Essence** is composed for the instruction of the young <note>original: "bāla-bodhāya," meaning "for the awakening of children" or beginners</note>.

In the year **1833** of the Vikrama Samvat era <note>approx. 1776 CE</note> and **1790** of the Shaka era <note>approx. 1868 CE; note that these dates do not align traditionally, suggesting either a later annotation or a specific regional reckoning</note>.

**Now follow the months:**
Chaitra, Vaishakha, Jyeshtha, Ashadha, Shravana, Bhadrapada, Ashvina, Kartika, Margashira, Pausha, Magha, and Phalguna—12 months in total.

**Now the fortnights:**
The dark half <note>original: "krishna-paksha," the waning moon</note> and the light half <note>original: "shukla-paksha," the waxing moon</note>.

**Now the lunar days:** <note>term: "tithi" — the 30 divisions of the lunar month</note>
The first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh, eighth, ninth, tenth, eleventh, twelfth, thirteenth, and fourteenth days; then the Full Moon <note>original: "purnima"</note> and the New Moon <note>original: "amavasya"</note>.

**Now the days of the week:**
Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, and Saturday—7 days.

**Now the lunar mansions:** <note>term: "nakshatra" — the 28 sectors of the ecliptic through which the moon passes</note>
1. Ashvini, 2. Bharani, 3. Krittika, 4. Rohini, 5. Mrigashira, 6. Ardra, 7. Punarvasu, 8. Pushya, 9. Ashlesha, 10. Magha, 11. Purva-Phalguni, 12. Uttara-Phalguni, 13. Hasta, 14. Chitra, 15. Svati, 16. Vishakha, 17. Anuradha, 18. Jyeshtha, 19. Mula, 20. Purva-Ashadha, 21. Uttara-Ashadha, 22. Abhijit, 23. Shravana, 24. Dhanishtha, 25. Shatabhisha, 26. Purva-Bhadrapada, 27. Uttara-Bhadrapada, 28. Revati.

**Now the zodiac signs:** <note>term: "rashi" — the twelve signs of the solar zodiac</note>
Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces—12 signs.

**[Phonetic Table for Naming]**
<note>This section maps specific sounds to the "quarters" of each lunar mansion to determine auspicious names for children born at those times.</note>
*Chu, Che, Cho, La* for **Ashvini**;
*Li, Lu, Le, Lo* for **Bharani**;
*A, I, U, E* for **Krittika**;
*O, Va, Vi, Vu* for **Rohini**;
*Ve, Vo, Ka, Ki* for **Mrigashira**;
*Ku, Gha, Nga, Cha* for **Ardra**;
*Ke, Ko, Ha, Hi* for **Punarvasu**;
*Hu, He, Ho, Da* for **Pushya**;
*Di, Du, De, Do* for **Ashlesha**;
*Ma, Mi, Mu, Me* for **Magha**;
*Mo, Ti, Tu, Te* for **Purva-Phalguni**;
*Te, To, Pa, Pi* for **Uttara-Phalguni**;
*E, Sha, Na, Tha* for **Hasta**;
*Pe, Po, Ra, Ri* for **Chitra**...

...hu, re, ro, tā, tā, tī <note>These are phonetic syllables associated with the quarters of the preceding lunar mansion.</note>. **Ti, tu, te, to** are for **Vishakha**. **Ni, nu, ne, no** are for **Anuradha**. **Ya, yi, yu, ye** are for **Jyeshtha**. **Ye, yo, bha, bhi** are for **Mula**. **Bhū, dhā, pha, dha** <note>OCR: 'shya phā phā e'</note> are for **Purvashadha**. **Be, bo, ja, ji** are for **Uttarashadha**. **Ju, je, jo, gha** are for **Abhijit** <note>Abhijit is an intercalary lunar mansion often placed between Uttarashadha and Shravana.</note>. **Khi, khu, khe, kho** are for **Shravana**. **Ga, gi, gu, ge** are for **Dhanishtha**. **Go, sa, si, su** are for **Shatabhisha**. **Se, so, da, di** are for **Purva Bhadrapada**. **Du, tha, jha, ña** <note>OCR: 'u ya tra u'</note> are for **Uttara Bhadrapada**. **De, do, ca, ci** are for **Revati**.

### Now, the Consideration of the Moon
<note>original: "Atha candravicāraḥ." This section explains how the 27 lunar mansions are grouped into the 12 zodiac signs.</note>

The four quarters of **Ashvini**, the four quarters of **Bharani**, and the first quarter <note>term: "pada" — a 'foot' or quarter; each mansion is divided into four equal parts</note> of **Krittika** constitute **Aries (1)**.

The remaining three quarters of **Krittika**, the four quarters of **Rohini**, and half <note>two quarters</note> of **Mrigashira** constitute **Taurus (2)**.

The remaining half of **Mrigashira**, the four quarters of **Ardra**, and three quarters of **Punarvasu** constitute **Gemini (3)**.

One quarter of **Punarvasu**, the four quarters of **Pushya**, and the four quarters of **Ashlesha** constitute **Cancer (4)**.

The four quarters of **Magha**, the four quarters of **Purva Phalguni**, and the first quarter of **Uttara Phalguni** constitute **Leo (5)**.

The remaining three quarters of **Uttara Phalguni**, the four quarters of **Hasta**, and half of **Chitra** constitute **Virgo (6)**.

The remaining half of **Chitra**, the four quarters of **Swati**, and three quarters of **Vishakha** constitute **Libra (7)**.

One quarter of **Vishakha**, the four quarters of **Anuradha**, and the four quarters of **Jyeshtha** constitute **Scorpio (8)**.

The four quarters of **Mula**, the four quarters of **Purvashadha**, and the first quarter of **Uttarashadha** constitute **Sagittarius (9)**.

The remaining three quarters of **Uttarashadha**, the four quarters of **Shravana**, and half of **Dhanishtha** constitute **Capricorn (10)**.

The remaining half of **Dhanishtha**, the four quarters of **Shatabhisha**, and three quarters of **Purva Bhadrapada** constitute **Aquarius (11)**.

One quarter of **Purva Bhadrapada**, the four quarters of **Uttara Bhadrapada**, and the four quarters of **Revati** constitute **Pisces (12)**.

### Now the Syllables of the Zodiac Signs
<note>original: "Atha rāśinakṣatrāṇi." This provides a quick reference for the naming syllables associated with each of the twelve signs.</note>

**Chu, che, cho, lā, li, lū, le, lo, a** — **Aries (1)**.
**I, u, e, o, va, vi, vu, ve, vo** — **Taurus (2)**.
**Ka, ki, ku, gha, ṅa, cha, ke, ko, ha** — **Gemini (3)**.
**Hi, hu, he, ho, dā, di, du, de, do** — **Cancer (4)**.
**Ma, mi, mu, me, mo, ṭā, ṭi, ṭu, ṭe** — **Leo (5)**.
**Ṭo, pa, pi, pu, ṣa, ṇa, ṭha, pe, po** — **Virgo (6)**.
**Rā, ri, ru, re, ro, tā, ti, tu, te** — **Libra (7)**.
**To, na, ni, nu, ne, no, ya, yi, yu** — **Scorpio (8)**.
**Ye, yo, bha, bhi, bhu, dhā, pha, dha, bhe** <note>OCR is slightly irregular here</note> — **Sagittarius (9)**.
**Bho, ja, ji, ju, je, jo, khā, khi, khu, khe, kho, ga, gi** — **Capricorn (10)**.
**Ge, go, sā, si, su, se, so, da** — **Aquarius (11)**.
**Di, du, thā, jha, ña, de, do, ca, ci** — **Pisces (12)**.

# The Twelve Signs and the Directions of the Moon
<note>original: "mīna 12 atha candradiśā"</note>

In the signs of **Aries (1)**, **Leo (5)**, and **Sagittarius (9)**, the Moon resides in the **East**.
In **Taurus (2)**, **Virgo (6)**, and **Capricorn (10)**, it resides in the **South** <note>original: "yāmyā" — an epithet for the South, the direction of Yama, the god of death</note>.
In **Gemini (3)**, **Libra (7)**, and **Aquarius (11)**, it resides in the **West**.
In **Cancer (4)**, **Scorpio (8)**, and **Pisces (12)**, it resides in the **North**.

### The Results of the Moon's Position
Now, the results: If the Moon is positioned **in front**, there is **gain of wealth**. If to the **right**, there is **happiness and prosperity**. If it is **behind**, it brings **death** itself. If the Moon is to the **left**, it causes **loss of wealth**.

# On Ritual Clothing
Now, regarding clothing:
For **Aries** and **Leo**, one should record **Red**.
For **Gemini** and **Virgo**, **Yellow** clothing.
For **Taurus** and **Cancer**, the appearance is **White**.
In **Aquarius**, **Pisces**, and **Capricorn**, the result is **Black**.

**The Omens of Colors:**
In **red** clothing, there may be **battle**.
In **yellow** clothing, there is a mix of **good and bad**.
In **white** clothing, there will be **gain**.
In **black**, there is a **doubt of death** <note>meaning a danger to one's life</note>.

# The Movement of the Yogini
<note>The Yogini is a celestial goddess who moves through the eight directions according to the lunar day (tithi). Traveling towards her is considered highly inauspicious.</note>

On the **1st** and **9th** lunar days, she is in the **East** (Indra's quarter).
In the **North-East**, she is on the **New Moon** (30th) and the **8th**.
On the **3rd** and **11th**, she is in the **South-East**.
In the **South**, she is on the **5th** and **13th**.
On the **12th** and **4th**, the Yogini is always in the **South-West**.
On the **6th** and **14th**—
<margin>In the West</margin> —she resides in the **West**.
On the **7th** and the **Full Moon** (15th), the Yogini resides in the **North-West**.
On the **2nd** joined with the **10th**, the auspicious one <note>original: "śivā" — a reference to the Yogini/Goddess</note> dwells in the **North**.

### The Result of the Yogini's Position
Now, the results of the Yogini: To the **left** or **behind**, she is the **giver of desires**. To the **right**, she is the **destroyer of wealth**. If she is in the **front** (facing you), she is the **giver of death**.
Whether at **home** or on a **journey**, one should not face her or keep her to the right. This should be known through the science of calculations <note>original: "gāna śāstre" — though "gāna" often means song, in this context it likely refers to the "ganita" or calculation branch of astrology</note>.

# The Directional Spear
<note>term: "diśā śūlam" — literally 'direction-spear'; these are directions that are 'pierced' or forbidden for travel on specific days of the week.</note>

On **Saturday** and **Monday**, the spear is in the **South-West**.
On **Thursday**, it is in the **South**.
On **Sunday** and **Friday**, it is in the **West**.
On **Wednesday** and **Tuesday**, it is in the **North** <note>The text here is somewhat condensed and elliptical</note>.

# The Lords of the Zodiac Signs
<note>original: "rāśi svāminaḥ"</note>

| Sign | Ruling Planet |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Aries** & **Scorpio** | **Mars** <note>original: "bhauma"</note> |
| **Taurus** & **Libra** | **Venus** <note>original: "śukra"</note> |
| **Virgo** & **Gemini** | **Mercury** <note>original: "budha"</note> |
| **Cancer** | **Moon** <note>original: "candramāḥ"</note> |
| **Sagittarius** & **Pisces** | **Jupiter** <note>original: "jīva"</note> |
| **Capricorn** & **Aquarius** | **Saturn** <note>original: "śani"</note> |
| **Leo** | **Sun** <note>original: "sūrya"</note> |

This has been declared by the **best of calculators**.

# The Alphabetical Groups
Now, the groups: A, I, U, E, Ga, Ru, U... <note>The text cuts off here as it begins the Varga system, which assigns letters of the alphabet to specific astrological influences.</note>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on astrological timing. While the previous page focused on the Moon’s position and the omens associated with the color of ritual clothing, this page introduces the "Varga" animal classifications for the Sanskrit alphabet and provides a detailed guide to the movement of Bhadra—a specific astrological period considered inauspicious for most earthly activities.</meta>

# The Animal Classifications of the Alphabet
The Sanskrit consonants are divided into groups <note>vargas</note>, each associated with a symbolic animal. These are used to determine compatibility and character.

| Consonant Group | Symbolic Animal |
| :--- | :--- |
| **ka, kha, ga, gha, ṅa** | **Falcon/Bird** (1) |
| **ca, cha, ja, jha, ña** | **Lion** (3) |
| **ṭa, ṭha, ḍa, ḍha, ṇa** | **Dog** (4) |
| **ta, tha, da, dha, na** | **Serpent** (5) |
| **pa, pha, ba, bha, ma** | **Rat** (6) |
| **ya, ra, la, va** | **Boar/Deer** <note>original: "shurana"</note> (7) |
| **śa, ṣa, sa, ha** | **Sheep/Ram** (8) |

Regarding these groups: A group is considered a **Friend** <note>mitra</note> to its own group and the fifth or fourth group from it. It is considered **Neutral** <note>udāsīna</note> toward the third group. Other combinations are viewed as conflicting.

# On Bhadra (The Inauspicious Time)
Now, the rules regarding **Bhadra** <note>Bhadra, also known as Vishti, is a period in the lunar calendar where the "shadow" of the day's energy is active. It is generally avoided for auspicious beginnings like weddings or travel.</note>. According to the sage **Kashinatha** in the text *Shighra-bodha*:

### Where Bhadra Resides
Bhadra travels through the three worlds—Heaven, the Netherworld, and Earth—depending on the sign the Moon is currently occupying.

*   **In Heaven:** When the Moon is in **Aries, Taurus, Gemini, or Scorpio**, Bhadra resides in the **Heavenly Realm** <note>Swarga-loka</note>.
*   **In the Netherworld:** When the Moon is in **Virgo, Libra, Sagittarius, or Capricorn**, Bhadra resides in the **Serpent Realm** <note>Naga-loka</note>.
*   **On Earth:** When the Moon is in **Cancer, Aquarius, Pisces, or Leo**, Bhadra resides in the **Mortal Realm** <note>Martya-loka</note>.

**The Result:** When Bhadra is in Heaven or the Netherworld, she is favorable to those on Earth. However, when she resides on Earth, her presence is "fruitful" <note>meaning her negative effects are active</note> and all auspicious work should be abandoned.

### The Timing of Bhadra (Vishti Karana)
Bhadra occurs during specific halves of the lunar days <note>tithis</note>:

**During the Dark Fortnight:**
*   On the **3rd** and **10th** lunar days, she occurs in the **second half** of the day.
*   On the **7th** and **14th** lunar days, she occurs in the **first half** of the day.

**During the Light Fortnight:**
*   On the **4th** and **11th** lunar days, she occurs in the **second half** of the day.
*   On the **8th** and **15th (Full Moon)** lunar days, she occurs in the **first half** of the day.

### Activities and Bhadra
One should avoid the following when Bhadra is on Earth:
Entering a home, meeting a King, entering a forest, travel, the arrival of a physician, crossing water, using weapons, or performing acts of "uprooting" <note>uccatana — a magical ritual to remove an enemy</note>.
However, actions involving lions, camels, donkeys, buffaloes, or the use of specific medicines and eye-salves <note>anjana</note> are said to be always favored by Bhadra.

# The Numerical Calculations
<note>This section uses symbolic words to represent numbers, a common practice in ancient Sanskrit mathematics and astrology.</note>
The "Veda" counts are as follows:
**Moon (1), Elements (5), Planets (9), Eyes (2), Tastes (6), Directions (10), Fire (3), Oceans (4),** as well as **Vedas (4), Eightfold (8), Shiva (11), and Suns (12).**

The results of these counts are:
1.  In the first: **Sorrow**
2.  In the second: <unclear>Success</unclear>
3.  In the third: **Gaining Wealth**
4.  In the fourth: **Quarrels**
5.  In the fifth: **Increase of Children**
6.  In the sixth: **Peace**

### On the Effects of the Planets
Regarding the birth chart <note>original: "dvijanmani"</note>: when the Sun, Rahu, Saturn, and Mars <note>original: "bhumisuta," literally "son of the Earth"</note> are situated in the first (1), fifth (5), seventh (7), fourth (4), eighth (8), or twelfth (12) houses, or are combined with the fifth (5) house of religious merit—if they do not move toward a favorable position, they cause the destruction of gold and wealth.

### On the Measurements for Women
Regarding the measurement of gentle and fierce planetary influences for women: in Pisces and Aries, three units are highly auspicious. In Taurus and Aquarius, [the measurement is similar]. In Gemini and Capricorn, five units are prescribed. In the ascendant <note>lagna</note>, six units are remembered as the standard.

### On the Yogas
<note>In Indian astrology, a "Yoga" is one of 27 specific divisions of time based on the combined longitude of the Sun and Moon. Each has a unique character.</note>
The Yogas are: **Vishkumbha** (The Support), **Priti** (Love), **Ayushman** (The Long-lived), **Saubhagya** (Good Fortune), **Shobhana** (Splendor), **Atiganda** (Great Danger), **Sukarma** (Virtuous Action), **Dhriti** (Resolution), **Shula** (The Spear), **Ganda** (The Knot), **Vriddhi** (Growth), **Dhruva** (The Fixed), **Vyaghata** (The Blow), **Harshana** (Joy), **Vajra** (The Thunderbolt), **Siddhi** (Success), **Vyatipata** (Calamity), **Variyan** (The Superior), **Parigha** (The Obstacle), **Shiva** (The Auspicious), **Siddha** (The Accomplished), **Sadhya** (The Achievable), **Shubha** (The Bright), **Shukla** (The White), **Brahma** (The Creator), **Indra** (The Chief), and **Vaidhriti** (The Disturbance). These twenty-seven are named as having significant results.

### On the Consideration of the Karana
<note>A "Karana" is half of a Tithi (lunar day). There are 11 types, divided into "mobile" and "stationary."</note>
Double the current lunar day <note>tithi</note>, subtract one, and divide the result by seven. The remainder is called the **Karana**.
The seven mobile Karanas are:
1. **Bava**
2. **Balava**
3. **Kaulava**
4. **Taitila**
5. **Gara**
6. **Vanija**
7. **Vishti**

Regarding the stationary Karanas: **Shakuni** occurs in the second half of the 14th day of the dark fortnight. **Chatushpada** and **Naga** occur during the two halves of the New Moon day <note>Amavasya</note>. **Kimstughna** occurs during the first half of the first day of the bright fortnight. These four are fixed, as the wise have taught. Following the end of the first day of the bright fortnight, the **Bava** Karana begins again. These eleven should be understood through their division into mobile and stationary types.

### On the Lords of the Nakshatras
<note>Each of the 27 lunar mansions (Nakshatras) is presided over by a Vedic deity.</note>
The lords are: The **Ashwins** <note>Dasra</note>, **Agni** (Fire), **Soma** (The Moon), **Rudra**, **Aditi**, **Brihaspati** (The Guru), the **Serpents** <note>Bhujangama</note>, the **Ancestors** <note>Pitris</note>, **Bhaga**, **Aryaman**, **Savitri** (The Sun), **Tvashta**, **Vayu** (The Wind), **Indra-Agni**, **Mitra**, **Indra** <note>Shakra</note>, **Nirriti**, **Apas** (The Waters), the **Vishvadevas**, the **Vasus**, **Varuna**, and **Aja-Ekapada**...

...the wise have declared twenty-eight <note>original: "ashtavimshati," referring to the 27 standard lunar mansions plus the intercalary star Abhijit</note> to be the number [of lunar mansions]. Now, the auspicious alignments <term>Siddhayoga|An auspicious astrological period where actions undertaken are likely to succeed</term> based on the lunar mansions, lunar days, and weekdays are as follows: 

On a Sunday, the stars **Ashwini**, **Hasta**, **Mula**, **Pushya**, and the three **Uttaras** <note>The "three Uttaras" are the stars Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> create the **Yoga of Achievement**. Likewise, the fifth lunar day <note>Panchami</note> also creates the **Yoga of Achievement**. 

On a Sunday, the stars **Vishakha**, **Bharani**, **Jyeshtha**, **Anuradha** <note>original: "maitra"</note>, and **Magha**, combined with the fourteenth, twelfth, or seventh lunar days, create the **Opposing Alignments** <term>Viruddha Yoga|Inauspicious planetary combinations that should be avoided for important tasks</term>. 

On a Monday, the stars **Pushya**, the three **Uttaras**, **Shravana**, **Rohini**, and **Mrigashirsha**, along with the ninth or tenth lunar days, are also declared by the wise to be the **Yoga of Achievement**. 

On a Monday <note>original: "candre," literally "under the Moon"</note>, the stars **Vishakha**, **Bharani**, and **Purva Phalguni**, combined with the eleventh, sixth, or thirteenth lunar days, are the **Opposing Alignments** that should be avoided. 

On a Saturday <note>original: "shanau"</note>, the stars **Rohini**, **Swati**, **Shravana**, **Purva Phalguni**, and **Magha**, combined with the ninth, fourth, or fourteenth lunar days, create the **Yoga of Achievement**. 

On a Saturday, the stars **Hasta**, **Uttara Ashadha**, **Vishakha**, **Purva Phalguni**, and **Revati**—these should always be avoided [for important work]—along with the sixth or seventh lunar days, create the **Opposing Alignments**. || 6 ||

**Nanda**: 1st, 6th, and 11th lunar days.
**Bhadra**: 2nd, 7th, and 12th lunar days.
**Jaya**: 3rd, 8th, and 13th lunar days.
**Rikta**: 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days.
**Purna**: 5th, 10th, and 15th <note>Full Moon/New Moon</note> lunar days.

The wise say that a **Nanda** day on a Friday, **Bhadra** on a Saturday, **Rikta** on a Tuesday, **Jaya** on a Wednesday, and **Purna** on a Thursday are to be known as the **Yoga of Achievement**; in auspicious matters, they bring auspicious results.

# The All-Achieving Alignment
The star **Hasta** on a Sunday, **Mrigashirsha** on a Monday, **Ashwini** on a Tuesday, **Anuradha** <note>original: "maitram"</note> on a Wednesday, **Pushya** on a Thursday, **Revati** on a Friday, and **Rohini** on a Saturday <note>original: "surya putre," literally "Son of the Sun"</note> grants all manner of success. This has been called the **Alignment of the Nectar of Success** <term>Amrutasiddhi|A highly auspicious period believed to make one's efforts "immortal" or fruit-bearing</term> by the ancients. **Thus ends the Alignment of the Nectar of Success.**

When a certain lunar day is joined with a certain weekday, if the thirteenth day is involved, it becomes the **Saw Alignment** <term>Krakachayoga|An inauspicious time likened to a saw, which "cuts" or destroys the benefits of an action</term>, which destroys auspiciousness. **Thus ends the Saw Alignment.**

**Magha** on a Sunday, **Vishakha** on two [days], [and] on a Tuesday... <note>The text cuts off mid-calculation of further alignments.</note>

The rule is as follows: on a Friday, the star **Bharani** <note>original: "yama," the deity presiding over Bharani</note> creates a specific alignment; on a Saturday, the star **Hasta** <note>original: "kara," a synonym for Hasta</note> creates the Saturday-Yama alignment <term>Chatayoga|An inauspicious planetary combination often associated with destruction or loss</term>. 

The **Nanda** <note>The 1st, 6th, and 11th lunar days</note> days fall on Sunday and Tuesday; the **Bhadra** <note>The 2nd, 7th, and 12th lunar days</note> days fall on Friday and Monday; and the **Jaya** <note>The 3rd, 8th, and 13th lunar days</note> days fall on Wednesday. 

The **Rikta** <note>The 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, literally "empty" days</note> days fall on Saturday, and the **Purna** <note>The 5th, 10th, and 15th lunar days, literally "full" days</note> day is the "giver of death" <note>original: "mritada"</note>. On a Saturday, the star **Revati** creates the **Alignment of Death** <term>Mrityu Yoga|A highly inauspicious time believed to bring failure or health risks</term>. 

The stars **Anuradha** <note>original: "maitra"</note>, the three stars starting with **Hasta** <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Svati</note>, **Mrigashirsha**, and **Punarvasu** are to be known [for specific rites]. Under the Sun's influence, these stars bring the seed of form and allow one to accomplish **Universal Success** <note>original: "sarvasiddhi"</note>. 

The three **Purvas** <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, along with **Vishakha**, **Krittika**, **Bharani** <note>original: "yama"</note>, **Mula**, and **Svati** <note>original: "vayu"</note> are known by the wise as a group of nine stars. <margin>7</margin> Earthworks, fire rituals, digging pits or holes, and warfare should be performed while the "face of the wind" <note>likely referring to downward-facing stars</note> is active; the wise should conduct such works then. 

The three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Pushya**, **Rohini**, **Ardra**, and the three stars starting with **Shravana** <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha; original: "shruti trayam"</note> are known as the **Upward-facing Stars** <term>Urdhva-mukha|Stars whose influence is directed upwards, suitable for vertical constructions and growth</term> by the learned. 

Palaces, ceremonial umbrellas, houses, city walls, flags, and arched gateways—as well as royal consecrations and the chanting of mantras—should be performed under this **Upward-facing** group of stars. 

The [twenty-eight alignments] are: **Ananda**, **Kaladanda**, **Dhumra**, **Dhatri**, **Saumya**, **Dhvanksha**, **Ketaka**, and then **Shrivatsa**, **Vajra**, **Mudgara**, **Chhatra**, **Maitra**, **Pramada**, **Karala**, **Siddhi**, **Shubha**, **Amrita**, **Musala**, **Gada**, **Matanga**, **Rakshasa**, **Chara**, **Sthira**, and **Vardhamana**. These grant fruits according to their names. <margin>7 in Mudgara; 5 names in Veda; 4 names in Padma; 7 in Gada; 4 in Musala; 2; 1; 2. These are the Death-bringers; all others are the Ananda alignments.</margin>

Using the calculation of the "head" and "tail" <note>original: "vesha-pucchah," referring to specific beginning and end points in astrological mathematics</note> and the ninth lunar day, the scholar determines the timing of the alignment. 

The **Threefold Success Alignment** <term>Traipushkara Yoga|A potent alignment where any action taken—whether for gain or loss—is said to be tripled in its result</term> occurs when a **Bhadra** <note>The 2nd, 7th, or 12th lunar days</note> day falls on a Saturday, Tuesday, or Sunday, and coincides with the stars **Krittika**, **Punarvasu**, **Uttarashadha**, **Vishakha**, **Uttara Phalguni**, or **Uttara Bhadrapada** <note>original: "dizharddhamaja-caranaditi-vadvivaisvai"</note>. This alignment is significant for the increase or destruction of one's essence; it is said that even the moon smiles upon this threefold configuration.

The **Solar Alignment** <term>Ravi Yoga|An auspicious solar alignment believed to dispel a multitude of negative influences and "doshas"</term> is determined by counting from the Sun's current star to the Moon's star in measures of 4, 9, 6, 10, 12, or 20. This alignment acts as a destroyer of accumulated flaws and misfortunes.

### Classification of Lunar Days (Tithis)

The lunar days are categorized into five groups which govern the success of different types of activities:

| Group Name | Lunar Days (Tithis) | Nature |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Nanda** (Joyful) | 1st, 6th, 11th | Beneficial for happiness |
| **Bhadra** (Fortunate) | 2nd, 7th, 12th | Good for starting new works |
| **Jaya** (Victorious) | 3rd, 8th, 13th | Best for overcoming obstacles |
| **Rikta** (Empty) | 4th, 9th, 14th | Generally avoided for new ventures |
| **Purna** (Full) | 5th, 10th, 15th | Excellent for completion |

### The Nature of the Stars (Nakshatras)

The stars are classified by their inherent "temperament," which dictates what earthly actions they best support:

*   **Movable or Changeable** <note>original: "cara-calams"</note>: **Svati**, **Punarvasu**, and the three stars beginning with **Shravana** <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhisha</note>.
*   **Cruel or Harsh**: **Bharani**, **Magha**, and the three stars beginning with **Purva-Phalguni** <note>Purva-Phalguni, Purvashadha, and Purva-Bhadrapada</note>.
*   **Fixed and Permanent** <note>original: "dhruve-sthira"</note>: **Rohini** and the three "Uttara" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttarashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>.
*   **Sharp or Dreadful**: **Ashlesha**, **Jyeshtha**, **Ardra**, and **Mula**.
*   **Swift and Light**: **Pushya**, **Hasta**, **Ashwini**, and **Abhijit**.
*   **Gentle and Friendly**: **Chitra**, **Anuradha**, **Revati**, and **Mrigashirsha**.
*   **Mixed or Ordinary**: **Vishakha** and **Krittika**. 

One should perform deeds that correspond to the specific "name" and quality of these stars.

### Divine Lords of the Lunar Days

Each lunar day is presided over by a specific deity, who grants their blessing to the day's activities:
The First day is led by **Gauri**; the Second by **Ganesha**; the Third by **Hari** <note>Vishnu</note>; the Fourth by **Guha** <note>Kartikeya</note>; the Fifth by **Ishvara** <note>Shiva</note>; the Sixth by **Vighna**; the Seventh by **Mahesha**; the Eighth by **Durga**; the Ninth by **Antaka** <note>Yama, the god of death</note>; the Tenth by **Vishvadeva**; the Eleventh by **Kamesha** <note>the god of love</note>; the Twelfth by **Soma** <note>the Moon</note>; and the Thirteenth by the **Ancestors** <note>original: "pitarau"</note>.

Finally, for matters of compatibility and merit, one must examine the eight criteria: **Varna** <note>class</note>, **Vashya** <note>dominance</note>, **Tara** <note>star strength</note>, **Yoni** <note>animal affinity</note>, **Graha-maitri** <note>planetary friendship</note>, **Gana** <note>temperament</note>, **Bhakuta** <note>relative sign position</note>, and **Nadi** <note>pulse/constitution</note>. These qualities increase the points of agreement between individuals.

<summary>This page presents a detailed grid for calculating the twenty-eight Anandadi Yogas, which are astrological alignments determined by the combination of the weekday and the Moon's position in the Nakshatras. Each alignment carries a specific qualitative result, ranging from highly auspicious (Amrita, Siddhi) to dangerous (Mrityu, Rakshasa).</summary>

# Classifications of the Zodiac and the Nature of Signs

Without being joined to the world of the unseen, one observes the signs beginning with Aries, Taurus, Leo, and the front half of Sagittarius. <note>original: "meshavrishanisimhamratashtva"</note> Cancer is known as a creature of the water; Scorpio is a reptile. The second half of Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces are famously known as water-dwellers. Gemini, Libra, Sagittarius (first half), and Virgo are declared to be bipeds. Such are the divisions according to their nature.

Regarding the affinity of the classes <term>Varna|A system of four categories—Priest, Warrior, Merchant, and Laborer—used here to classify the 'temperament' of a zodiac sign</term>, one should calculate from the sign of the bridegroom up to the sign of the Virgo (the bride). If the remainder is divided by nine, the three results determine the suitability.

Now, the stars <term>Nakshatra|The 27 lunar mansions or star constellations through which the moon passes</term> and their animal spirits <term>Yoni|A classification system in Indian astrology where each star is assigned an animal archetype to determine physical and temperamental compatibility</term>:
Ashvini and Shatabhisha are Horses. Bharani and Revati are the Elephant. Pushya and Krittika are the Goat. Rohini and Mrigashira <note>original: "go nagashcha... mriga"</note> are the Serpent and the Deer. Ardra and Mula are the Mongoose. Magha and Purva Phalguni are the Cat. <note>original: "phalgunimaccha marjjaroditara"</note> Uttara Phalguni is the Cow. The three "Uttara" stars are the Buffalo. Swati and Hasta are the Buffalo <note>The text here lists Buffalo for Uttara and Swati/Hasta; traditionally Swati/Hasta are the Buffalo or Deer</note>. Vishakha is the Tiger.

### The Wheel of Social Class and Physical Stance

| Ari | Tau | Gem | Can | Leo | Vir | Lib | Sco | Sag | Cap | Aqu | Pis | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Warrior | Merchant | Laborer | Priest | Warrior | Merchant | Laborer | Priest | Warrior | Merchant | Laborer | Priest | <gloss>Wheel of Classes</gloss> |
| Ari | Tau | Gem | Can | Leo | Vir | Lib | Sco | Sag | Cap | Aqu | Pis | |
| Quadruped | Quadruped | Biped | Insect | Quadruped | Biped | Biped | Reptile | Quadruped | <unclear>Betadhvara</unclear> | Water-dweller | Water-dweller | |

->The divisions of Sagittarius are bipedal; the second half of Capricorn is a water-dweller.<-

The stars of Shravana and Purva Bhadrapada are the Monkey. Uttara Bhadrapada is the Lion. The Mongoose <note>original: "nakula"</note> is associated with Abhijit. These are the "Yonis" or biological origins of the stars. One must carefully consider the enmity <note>original: "vairam"</note> or friendship between them. 

Natural enemies include: the Cow and the Tiger; the Elephant and the Lion; the Horse and the Buffalo; the Dog and the Deer; the Serpent and the Mongoose; the Monkey and the Sheep.

This consideration of compatibility is vital for the world’s conduct and must be applied with great effort for couples, as well as for the relationship between a King and his servant. Those seeking auspiciousness should always avoid mismatched pairings. This concludes the section on Physical Compatibility <note>original: "Yoni Priti"</note>. Regarding planetary friendships: Saturn and Venus are enemies of the Sun, while the Moon is a friend, and the rest are neutral.

### On Planetary Relations and Temperament

The relationships of the planets are as follows: <gloss>the Sun</gloss> considers the Moon, Mars, and Jupiter as friends, while Mercury is neutral, and Venus and Saturn are enemies. For the Moon, the Sun and Mercury are friends, while the rest are neutral; it has no natural enemies. For Mars, the Sun, Moon, and Jupiter are friends, while Venus and Saturn are neutral, and Mercury is the enemy. For Mercury, the Sun and Venus are friends, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn are neutral, and the Moon is an enemy. For Jupiter, the Sun, Moon, and Mars are friends, Saturn is neutral, and Mercury and Venus are enemies. For Venus, Mercury and Saturn are friends, Mars and Jupiter are neutral, and the Sun and Moon are enemies. For Saturn, Mercury and Venus are friends, Jupiter is neutral, and the Sun, Moon, and Mars are enemies. These relationships are the basis for determining the strength of a planetary placement.

Regarding the three temperaments <term>Gana|A classification of the 27 stars into three groups: Divine (Deva), Human (Manushya), and Demon (Rakshasa), representing a person's basic nature or 'vibe'</term>:

The stars **Ashvini, Mrigashira, Punarvasu, Pushya, Hasta, Swati, Anuradha, Shravana, and Revati** are declared to be of the **Divine Temperament**. <note>original: "devataganah"</note>

The three "Purvas" (**Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada**), the three "Uttaras" (**Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada**), and the stars **Rohini and Bharani** are called the **Human Temperament**. <note>original: "manavī"</note>

The stars **Krittika, Magha, Ashlesha, Vishakha, Shatabhisha, Chitra, Jyeshtha, Dhanishta, and Mula** are known as the **Demon Temperament**. <note>original: "rakshasagana"</note>

In matchmaking, if both individuals belong to the same temperament, it is excellent. A match between the Divine and Human temperaments is considered average. However, a match between a Human and a Demon, or a Divine and a Demon, is said to lead to "Death or Constant Quarrel." <note>original: "mrityuh kalaho"</note> One who is of the Demon temperament is often seen as forceful or intense, and the Human temperament may feel overwhelmed or "blunted" by their energy. This is why such a union is generally avoided unless other planetary strengths compensate for the friction.

### The Effects of House Placements

Affection is found when signs are in the first house relationship, but in the sixth, there is enmity between the man and the woman. Therefore, the eighth house position is inauspicious. In the 2/12 relationship <term>Dvi-Dvadasha<note>A placement where one partner's moon sign is two houses away from the other's, who is conversely twelve houses away. It is often associated with financial loss.</note></term>, there is poverty; in the 9/5 relationship <term>Nava-Panchama<note>A relationship where signs are five and nine houses apart.</note></term>, there is strife. This 9/5 position may even foretell the death of the parents. 

However, if the bride’s sign is in the fifth house from the groom’s, and the groom’s is in the ninth from the bride’s, this <term>Trikona<note>A trine or triangular relationship, usually considered very auspicious in Vedic astrology.</note></term> arrangement is acceptable and brings the happiness of children and grandchildren. In the 6/8 relationship <term>Shadashtaka<note>original: "shada-ashtaka". This is considered one of the most dangerous combinations, suggesting fundamental incompatibility or "death" of the relationship.</note></term>, death occurs; therefore, one should examine this placement with great care. This concludes the section on Sign Compatibility <note>original: "Rashi Melana"</note>.

### The Nadi Chart

A serpent-shaped chart <note>original: "Sarpakara Chakram"</note> should be drawn to calculate the specific timings and Nadi in a marriage. One should consider the lunar mansions <note>original: "Nakshatras"</note> starting with Ashvini and the others. If a couple shares the same <term>Nadi<note>Literally "pulse" or "channel." In astrology, there are three Nadis: Adya (Initial), Madhya (Middle), and Antya (Final). Sharing the same Nadi creates a biological/energetic resonance that is traditionally thought to lead to health disasters for the couple.</note></term>, death will befall them. In service, it leads to loss; in marriage, it is simply inauspicious. 

The First Nadi kills the husband; the Middle Nadi kills the bride; and the Final Nadi causes barrenness. Therefore, one must avoid matching the same Nadi. Compatibility based on birth details is superior to compatibility based solely on names. If both partners share the same sign, it is said the village and household may be destroyed. However, the friendship between the planets <note>original: "Khechara Maitri"</note> is the most excellent of all compatibility tests. If the zodiac sign is the same but the lunar mansions are different, or if the sign is the same but the quarters <note>original: "Padas"</note> of the stars differ, then the Nadi fault is not considered a defect.

->इति नाडि चक्रम्<-
->This is the Nadi Chart<-

The results of the "vines" and fruits, friendship, the five-fold relationships, and the crossing of the planets <note>original: "Kranti"</note> should follow. Beware of the "burnt" lunar dates <note>original: "Dagda Tithi"</note>.

One should know that there are twenty-eight powerful defects <note>original: "doshas"</note>. The planets should then be placed on the grid according to where they are currently stationed in the sky. If a planet is situated near the ascendant or in the path of the lines, it creates a defect.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological obstructions, specifically focusing on 'Latta' (the 'kick' of a planet) and 'Pata' (the 'fall' or node). These are considered severe malefic influences that can ruin the success of a marriage or any major undertaking. The text provides specific geographical contexts where certain defects are considered more potent, reflecting regional variations in astrological practice.</meta>

Venus destroys all through its <term>Latta<note>Literally a "kick." In Jyotisha, this refers to a planet's ability to "kick" or afflict a specific lunar mansion (Nakshatra) located a certain distance from its current position.</note></term>. The kick of Venus brings loss, while the Moon <note>original: "Kalanidhi," meaning 'ocean of arts'</note> provides peace. This describes the Latta associated with the Sun.

The <term>Pata<note>Literally a "fall" or "node." This refers to specific astronomical points or moments of intersection that are considered spiritually and physically volatile.</note></term> of Rahu is now described. The stars Magha, Ashlesha, Chitra, Anuradha, and Revati should be counted starting from Ashwini. 

Fire, Wind, the Changeable, the Quarrelsome, Death, and the Thoughtful—these are the characteristics of the six types of Pata. By a Pata, even the creator Brahma falls; by a Pata, Vishnu <note>original: "Hari"</note> falls; by a Pata, Shiva <note>original: "Shambhu"</note> falls. Therefore, one must avoid the Pata at all costs. 3

<margin>Pushya/Seta?</margin> Regarding the Vyatipata defect: In the house where the Moon is situated, if a planet arrives there, an obstruction is known to occur, destroying both good and bad outcomes. A Latta of the Sun starting from Aries causes loss. Mars causes death. The Moon, Rahu, Ketu, and Saturn cause the destruction of the very foundation <note>original: "mula-nasha"</note>. So it is heard in the tradition. If a marriage occurs during these times, the couple will not live together even for a month.

Such are the defects. The Latta defect is avoided especially in the Malava country <note>Modern-day Malwa, Central India</note>. The Pata defect is avoided in the Kuru-Jangala region <note>The region around modern Delhi and Haryana</note>. The <term>Ekargala<note>A specific astronomical blockage or "one-spike" defect.</note></term> is avoided in Kashmir. However, the Vaidhruti defect must be avoided everywhere. 9, 13. Venus, Saturn, and Jupiter in certain placements... Rahu and Ketu act according to their own will. The fourteenth Nakshatra or the seventh house <note>original: "yamitra"</note> from the ascendant is noted; if joined by a benefic planet, it is desired, but if joined by a malefic, it should be avoided. The Moon, Venus, and Jupiter are auspicious makers in the seventh house. Rahu and the Sun in the seventh are also said to be beneficial <note>This may refer to specific exceptions to general rules</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical catalog of astrological obstructions. While the previous page focused on the 'kick' (Latta) and 'fall' (Pata) of planets, this page introduces the 'Arrows' (Banas), which are specific calculations used to determine if a day is auspicious for certain life events like marriage, travel, or starting a new job.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
<sig>Horoscope 8</sig>

| **The Malefic Arrows** | **Calculation Values** | **Day of the Week** | **Activity to Avoid/Note** |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Arrow of Disease** <note>original: "Rogabana"</note> | 8, 11, 15 | Sunday | Sacred thread ceremony <note>The rite of passage for students.</note> |
| **Arrow of Fire** <note>original: "Agnibana"</note> | 2, 11, 20 | Tuesday | Roofing a house |
| **Arrow of the King** <note>original: "Rajabana"</note> | 4, 13, 11 | Saturday | Royal service or employment |
| **Arrow of Thieves** <note>original: "Chaurabana"</note> | 8, 11, 14 | Tuesday | Traveling to foreign lands |
| **Arrow of Death** <note>original: "Mrityubana"</note> | 1, 10, 13, 18 | Wednesday | During the time of marriage |

Planets situated in those positions surely cause disease and widowhood. Thus, regarding the <term>Yamitra<note>The 7th house in a horoscope, which governs marriage and partnerships.</note></term> and the travel-related lunar days: take the days passed since the <term>Sankranti<note>The moment the Sun enters a new zodiac sign.</note></term> and add the current lunar date. Divide this by the number of planets. If the remainder results in the five-fold <note>original: "panchaka"</note> combinations, they are: disease, fire, king, thief, and death. Among these, the Arrows of the Fire and the King are considered auspicious, while the Arrow of Thieves and the Arrow of Disease are understood to be auspicious in specific sequences involving the third and seventh days from the Sankranti.

Now, regarding the odd and even numerical combinations for the <term>Panchaka<note>A period of five days or a calculation of five types of auspicious/inauspicious influences.</note></term>: twenty for the even and half for the odd. Similarly, in the calculation for the <term>Nakshatras<note>Lunar mansions or star constellations.</note></term>, one should place thirteen lines on the head... <note>This refers to a diagrammatic method of calculation used by astrologers.</note> If the resulting number reaches the specified count, it is called <term>Ekargala<note>A specific astrological defect (literally 'one bolt' or 'one bar') that occurs when the Sun and Moon are in certain positions, blocking the success of an undertaking.</note></term>. When the Sun and Moon join in this way, the Ekargala defect arises. Regarding the Sun's position, if the distance between the Nakshatras is 5, 6, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13, or 14, then a secondary planetary influence <note>original: "upagraha"</note> occurs.

| Ari | Tau | Gem | Can | Leo | Vir | Lib | Sco | Sag | Cap | Aqu | Pis | **Months** <note>The table lists the zodiac signs as months.</note> |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 6 | 4 | 8 | 9 | 10 | 8 | 12 | 10 | 2 | 12 | 4 | 2 | **Lunar Days** <note>original: "Tithis"</note> |

This is the rule. Thus ends the section on the <term>Upagrahas<note>Shadow planets or secondary mathematical points that exert influence.</note></term>. Having written this, one should write in the middle: for the sign of Pisces, the equality of declination <note>original: "kranti samya"</note> of the Sun and Moon is stated. This equality of declination occurs in Pisces and Sagittarius on the 2nd lunar day; in Taurus and Aquarius on the 4th; in Aries and Cancer on the 6th; in Virgo and Gemini on the 8th; in Scorpio on the 10th; and in Leo and Capricorn on the 12th.

<meta>This page continues the technical catalog of astrological obstructions, moving from the 'Arrows' (Banas) described previously to the 'Burnt Lunar Days' (Dagdha tithis) and the 'Scissor' formation (Kartari). It specifically focuses on their impact on choosing an auspicious time for marriage and calculating the vital planetary strengths for the bride and groom.</meta>

Below are the primary lunar days <note>original: "tithis"</note> that are considered the most significant; they are to be avoided during any auspicious work. These are known as the **Burnt Lunar Days** <note>original: "Dagdha tithi"</note>. They should be discarded in the <term>Ascendant<note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of an event, which sets the theme for the horoscope.</note></term>. If Saturn <note>original: "Manda"</note> is in the sixth house, the vessel of the diagram <note>original: "kunda-prapa"</note> falls.

->[Astrological Diagram of the Kunda (Grid)]<-
<note>This 3x3 grid helps practitioners visually locate planetary positions to identify "Burnt Days" and other malefic combinations.</note>

The eighth house <note>original: "randhra"</note> and the twelfth house—if all five planets are in the Ascendant, it is harsh. Where Saturn is in the eleventh, second, or third house, it is known as the **Cow-dust Hour** <note>original: "Godhalika"; traditionally the time of sunset when cows return home, considered a secondary auspicious time if others are unavailable.</note>. The others are known as the "mouths of the plate" <note>original: "thalimukha"</note>.

### The Scissor Formation
Now, regarding the **Scissor Formation** <note>original: "Kartari"</note>. When the sign is pressed between Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> and harsh planets <note>original: "khara-grahas," such as the Sun, Mars, or Saturn</note>, then a 'Scissor' occurs. It causes the failure of tasks and is not auspicious. This happens when the birth sign or the lord of the eighth house is in a debilitated sign. If it is aspected by benefics in the first, ninth, or fifth houses, or if the lord of the Ascendant is retrograde or in the tenth house, it is still called a 'Scissor' which brings forth distress and calamity. When the signs of Pisces, Scorpio, or Cancer are in the Ascendant and have a flaw, it creates a defect in the house.
**<term>Thus concludes the Deliberation on the Ascendant.</term>**

### Rules for Marriage
In the birth month, under the birth star, or on the birth day, or on a Wednesday—one should never perform a marriage in these times. In the month of <term>Jyeshtha<note>The third month of the Hindu lunar calendar, occurring around May and June.</note></term>, if both the bride and the groom are the eldest <note>original: "Jyeshtha"</note> children of their families, the "grasping of hands" (marriage) should not take place. However, if only one of the two is the eldest, no fault is incurred for the marriage in the month of Jyeshtha.

When Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> has entered the sign of Leo <note>original: "Simha"</note>, the marriage ceremony should never be performed. However, if the Sun <note>original: "Divanatha"</note> is situated in Aries, it is considered auspicious even if Jupiter is in Leo. At the end of a solar month (the final day) or the end of a lunar day <note>the last two or three *gharikas*, units of 24 minutes</note>, marriage should be avoided.

One must consider the strength of the Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note> for the groom and the strength of Jupiter for the bride. In marriage, the strength of both must be accepted; it should not be otherwise. If the Sun is in the twelfth house at the time of marriage, the groom will obtain death without a doubt. This applies to the birth star, the second, fifth, or seventh houses...

When the **Sun** <note>original: "Divanatha," literally "Lord of the Day"</note> is in the ninth house, there is affliction even by <unclear>Aja</unclear>. In the eleventh, third, sixth, and twelfth houses, the Sun is a giver of auspicious results for the **groom** <note>original: "vara"</note> in a marriage. The sages Garga, Gautama, Vashistha, Kashyapa, Parashara, and others say: if the Sun has entered the second house (associated with children), after thirteen days have passed, it becomes auspicious. This rule applies specifically to moveable signs. This concludes the **Strength of the Sun** <note>original: "Ravi-balam"</note>.

If **Jupiter** <note>original: "Brihaspati"</note> is in the eighth, twelfth, or fourth house, it is a destroyer of life for those not yet married. If in the sixth, the birth sign, or the seventh, or even the tenth, if abundant worship is performed, he becomes a bringer of good for the **bride** <note>original: "kanya"</note>. If in the eleventh, second, fifth, seventh, or ninth, the Teacher of the Gods <note>original: "Suracharya," an epithet for Jupiter</note> is a bringer of good for the bride. This concludes the **Strength of Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru-balam"</note>.

# Page 10

According to the astrologer Sasepa: This section concerns the **First Entry into New Clothes** <note>original: "Navavastra-pravesha"</note> and the timing for journeys. For the **Bride’s Return to her Home** <term>Dira-agamana-muhurta: the auspicious time for a newly married woman to return to her parental home for the first time or move between the two households</term>, the following are recommended: the constellations of Anuradha, Shravana, the two Dhanishthas, the three Hastas, the two Punarvasus, Pushya, and Ashwini. Also favorable are the three "Uttara" constellations <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>. The auspicious months are the odd-numbered months, specifically Margashirsha, Phalguna, and the signs of Aries and Taurus. The rising signs <note>Lagna</note> should be Capricorn, Gemini, Pisces, Virgo, or the first half of Libra. The 6th and 12th lunar days, the "Empty" days <term>Rikta: the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, traditionally considered hollow or unlucky for new beginnings</term>, and the New Moon must be avoided. This concludes the **Timing for the Bride’s Return**.

### Strength of the Sun and Jupiter (Continued)

For the groom, the **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi"</note> in the 4th, 8th, and 12th houses is inauspicious, while in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, it is auspicious. If the Sun is in other houses, it may be made auspicious through worship. For the **bride** <note>original: "kanya"</note>, **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> in the 2nd, 5th, 7th, and 9th houses is auspicious. In the 6th, 8th, and 12th, it is of medium strength, and in the 4th, 8th, and 12th, it is inauspicious.

<margin>For a Male Child. Hair-Parting.</margin> In the constellations of Ardra, the three Bhadrapadas, Mrigashirsha, Pushya, Ashwini, Hasta, Mula, and the three constellations ruled by Jupiter—avoiding Sundays, Tuesdays, and the 'Empty' lunar days—one should perform the **Ceremony for a Male Child** <term>Pumsavana: a ritual performed early in pregnancy to ensure the health of the fetus and the birth of a son</term> and the **Hair-Parting Ceremony** <term>Simanta: the "parting of the hair" ritual conducted for the well-being and mental health of the expectant mother</term>. This should be done when the month is Virgo or Taurus and the rising sign is fixed. This concludes the instructions for **Pumsavana** and **Simanta**.

### The Naming Ceremony

On the 11th or 12th day after birth, the **Naming Ceremony** <note>original: "Nama-karana"</note> should be performed. The auspicious constellations are the two Punarvasus, the two Hastas, Anuradha, Mula, the Uttaras, and Dhanishtha. Favorable days include Monday, Sunday, and Thursday, provided the rising sign is fixed. This concludes the **Naming Ceremony**.

### Wearing New Clothes and Jewelry

Regarding the wearing of gems, the five "Ratna" stones, and new clothes: the constellations Chitra, Dhanishtha, and Pushya are favorable. Thursday and Friday are the best days for women to wear new garments. The best rising signs are Pisces, Taurus, Virgo, and Gemini. For men, the constellations of Punarvasu, Rohini, and the Uttaras are recommended. This concludes the section on **Wearing New Clothes**.

#### Effects of the Days of the Week:
*   **Sunday:** Wearing new clothes causes eye disease or inflammation.
*   **Monday:** Causes distress or mental anxiety.
*   **Tuesday:** Causes destruction or danger of death (however, **red clothing** <note>original: "rakta-vastram"</note> is permitted for women even on this day).
*   **Wednesday:** Leads to the acquisition of knowledge and wealth.
*   **Thursday:** Brings various enjoyments and prosperity.
*   **Friday:** Leads to divine pleasure and comfort.
*   **Saturday:** Causes disease, sorrow, and quarrels.

### The First Feeding of Solid Food
Regarding the **First Feeding of Solid Food** <term>Annaprashana: a rite of passage marking a child's first intake of solid food</term>, the auspicious constellations are Ashlesha, Ardra, Shatabhisha, and Bharani <note>original: "Sarpa, Ada, Varuna, Yama"</note>. One should avoid the constellations near these. The forbidden days of the week are Tuesday and Saturday <note>original: "Bhaumarka-nandanan"</note>. One must also avoid the 12th and 7th lunar days, the "Empty" days <term>Rikta: the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered unlucky for new ventures</term>, and the transition days <term>Parva: the Full and New Moon days</term>.

The favorable rising signs <note>Lagna</note> are Aquarius, Taurus, Virgo, and Sagittarius. Venus should be in a strong position, the lunar fortnight should be bright, the astrological combination <term>Yoga</term> should be favorable, and the Moon must be auspicious during the Sun’s transit. For boys, the feeding should take place in the 6th or 8th month; for girls, it should be in the 5th month. **This concludes the section on the feeding of food.**

### Education and General Wellbeing
For general wellbeing <note>original: "Bhujanam"</note>, the constellations Punarvasu, Hasta, Jyeshtha, Mrigashira, Shravana, and Ashwini are recommended, especially when the rising sign is Taurus and the lunar day is auspicious in the sign of Capricorn.

Regarding the **Beginning of Education** <term>Vidyarambha: the traditional ceremony where a child is first taught to write the alphabet</term>, the favorable constellations are the three "Hastas," Shravana, Mrigashira, Mula, and Ashwini. It should be performed on a Wednesday, Sunday, Thursday, or Friday. The most auspicious rising signs are Pisces or Sagittarius, and it should ideally take place in the child’s fifth year.

**This concludes the section on the beginning of education.**

### The Sacred Thread Ceremony
Regarding the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <term>Yajnopavita or Vratabandha: the initiation ritual for boys, marking their entrance into formal Vedic study</term>, the favorable constellations are Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Shravana, Jyeshtha, Mrigashira, and Pushya. It should be performed during the **Northern Transit of the Sun** <term>Uttarayana: the spiritually potent six-month period when the sun moves northward</term>.

The favorable lunar days are the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, and the 10th. The favorable planets are the Sun, Venus, Jupiter, the Moon, and Mercury. The rising signs should be Taurus, Leo, Virgo, or Gemini. This ritual brings the realization of the ultimate truth for the learned classes. **This concludes the section on the Sacred Thread.**

### Consecration of Public Works
For the **Consecration of Temples, Step-wells, Wells, and Ponds** <note>original: "Devalaya-vapi-kupa-tadaga"</note>, the constellations Mula, Punarvasu, Shravana, Mrigashira, and Hasta are favorable. One should avoid performing these on Friday, Saturday, Sunday, or Tuesday. Furthermore, the constellations Shatabhisha, Ashlesha, Vishakha, and Bharani must be avoided. The forbidden lunar days are the 12th, the "Empty" days, the 6th, the 8th, and the 1st. This timing applies to the installation of deities, the placement of gems, and the consecration of Narayana <note>a name for Lord Vishnu</note> in temples.

# Astrological Timings for Construction and Labor

### Consecration of Wells and Public Works
For the consecration of wells, ponds, and similar works <note>original: "vapi-kupa-tadi-pratishtha"</note>, the auspicious constellations are Uttaraphalguni, the three stars of Hasta <note>original: "hastatrayam"</note>, Mula, Revati, Anuradha <note>original: "Maitra"</note>, and Ashwini. 

The favorable rising signs <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign ascending on the eastern horizon at a specific time</term> for these works are Leo, Virgo, Libra, Taurus, Gemini, and Capricorn. For rituals concerning children, the experts recommend the months of Shravana, Vaishakha, Kartika, Phalguna, and Margashirsha. These same months are also praised for **architectural work** <term>Vastu Karma: the traditional Hindu system of architecture</term>. 

One must avoid the inauspicious astrological combinations <term>Yoga</term> known as Vajra, Vyaghata, Shula, Vyatipata, and Gandaka, as these bring obstacles and danger. The most favorable days of the week are Tuesday, Wednesday, and Sunday <note>original: "Bhaum-Jna-Bhaskarai"</note>. 

**This concludes the section on the Beginning of House Construction.**

### Entering the New Home
When **Entering the House for the First Time** <term>Griha Pravesha: a ceremony performed when first inhabiting a new dwelling</term>, the constellations Vishakha, Bharani, and Magha should be avoided. One should also avoid the New Moon <note>original: "Amavasya"</note>, the "Empty" days <term>Rikta: the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered hollow or unlucky</term>, and the days of Mars and the Sun <note>Tuesday and Sunday</note>. 

The most favorable months for entry are Vaishakha, Margashirsha, and Pausha. The month of Jyeshtha is generally forbidden <note>original: "grasah"</note>. Wise men recommend a "fixed" rising sign <note>Sthira Lagna: signs like Taurus or Leo that represent stability</note> for this ritual. 

**This concludes the section on Entering the House.**

### The First Plowing
For the **First Plowing of the Field** <term>Hala Pravaha: the ritual commencement of the agricultural season</term>, the favorable constellations are Anuradha, Magha, Punarvasu <note>original: "Aditi-yugma"</note>, Hasta, Swati, and Revati. The plowing should be performed with a pair of oxen. One must avoid Saturday <note>original: "Shani"</note>, which is considered a cruel day for this work. Also avoided are the 6th, 12th, and 2nd lunar days, the "Empty" days, and the transition days <term>Parva: the Full and New Moon</term>. 

One should count the constellations starting from the Sun’s current position to determine the specific effects on the harvest. 

**This concludes the section on Plowing.**

### Travels and Holy Days
Regarding **Journeys** <note>original: "Yatra"</note>, the constellations Anuradha, Hasta, Mrigashira, Punarvasu, Revati, Ashwini, Ardra, Bharani, Magha, and Vishakha are considered moderate to good. The favorable rising signs are Virgo, Taurus, and Libra. Before setting out, one must consider the omens <note>original: "shakune"</note>. 

The five sacred transition days <note>original: "pancha-parvani"</note> are the 14th day, the 8th day, the Dark Moon, the Full Moon, and the day the Sun enters a new zodiac sign <term>Sankranti</term>. On these days, one should focus on spiritual strength rather than worldly travel or new ventures.

**Concerning Travel.** One should avoid all travel toward the South <note>original: "dakshinam cha disham"</note> when the Moon or Saturn are in certain positions. Avoid the West when Jupiter, the Sun, or Venus are present. Avoid the North when Mercury or Mars are positioned there. **This concludes the section on Obstacles of the Directions.**

The <term>Yogini: a semi-divine spirit or energy whose position in the sky changes daily</term> provides success when she is to the left <note>original: "vame"</note>. When she is behind the traveler <note>original: "prishthe"</note>, she grants one's desires. When she is to the right, she brings one before a king or great leader. However, when she is directly in front, she brings the threat of death. **This concludes the section on the Yogini.**

Regarding the direction of the planets in the sky: the wind-like movements of the planets are powerful. Success in all works comes when the teacher <note>referring to Jupiter</note> is behind. When <term>Shukra: the planet Venus</term> is to the right, it is said to be unlucky. When Venus is in front, it is like the "Three-eyed One" <note>original: "ran-ti-lochana," an epithet of Shiva; here implying a fierce or burning gaze</note>. It is auspicious when Venus is to the left or behind. One should check for the setting of planets to ensure a favorable outcome. **This concludes the section on Venus.**

When Venus is behind, and during the <term>Nakshatra: the 27 lunar mansions or star constellations through which the moon passes</term> of Bhadrapada, Shravana, Ashwini, Hasta, Uttaraphalguni, Mrigashira, Anuradha <note>original: "Maitra"</note>, Magha, or Revati—these are the stars to be chosen by the wise for **Buying and Selling** <term>Kraya-Vikraya: commercial transactions and trade</term>. The best days are Monday, Friday, and Thursday <note>original: "Chandra-Bhargava-Jiva"</note>, with Friday being the most excellent. **This concludes the section on Buying and Selling.**

Concerning the "Five Nights" to be avoided, here is a summary of the eleven days. One should avoid the southern direction when covering the roof of a house. Similarly, regarding **Oil Massage** <term>Tailabhyanga: the traditional practice of anointing the body with oil</term>: applying oil on a Sunday causes feverish heat; on Monday, it brings beauty; on Tuesday, it leads to death; on Wednesday, it causes the loss of wealth; on Thursday, it brings profit; on Friday, it brings wealth; and on Saturday, it brings happiness. 

If one must apply oil on an unfavorable day, use these remedies to pacify the negative effects: on Sunday, use flowers; on Tuesday, use earth/clay; on Thursday, use cow dung; and on Friday, use clarified butter <note>original: "ghritam"</note>. **This concludes the section on Oil Massage.**

For the **Bathing of a Sick Person**, the constellations Punarvasu, Rohini, and Swati are recommended. One should avoid Revati and the three "Uttara" stars. Avoid the "Empty" lunar days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th tithis</note>, and the days of the Sun and Mars. For those who are ill, bathing should be combined with the feeding of Brahmins. **This concludes the section on Bathing for the Sick.**

For taking **Medicine** <note>original: "Aushadham"</note>, the constellations Punarvasu, Ashwini <note>original: "Hayesha"</note>, Mrigashira, and the three "Uttara" stars are best. Mula, Shravana, and Anuradha <note>original: "Maitra"</note> are also praised for medical works. Monday and Friday are the best days. Among the rising signs <note>Lagna</note>, Pisces and Taurus are acceptable. **This concludes the section on Medicine.**

One must avoid the New Moon <note>Amavasya</note>, the 8th day, and the 14th day of the lunar cycle. On a Sunday, one must also avoid the combination known as "Death-Yoga" <note>original: "Yogo-yam-mritam"</note>...

At the time of departure, the <term>Nakshatras: lunar mansions or star constellations</term> of Chitra, the three Uttaras <note>Uttaraphalguni, Uttarashadha, and Uttarabhadrapada</note>, Rohini, and Shravana should also be avoided. When these six occur, it will be inauspicious for the various classes of people to depart. **Thus ends the section on Departure.**

One should not bring cattle home when the Moon is in the Uttara, Vishakha, Rohini, or Punarvasu constellations. Furthermore, it is not auspicious to bring them on the ninth, fourteenth, or eighth <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>. **Thus ends the section on the Entrance of Cattle.**

The constellations of Purva <note>the three "early" mansions: Purvaphalguni, Purvashadha, and Purvabhadrapada</note>, Maitra <note>Anuradha</note>, Ashvini <note>referred to here as "Haya" or horse</note>, Mula, Vasu <note>Dhanishtha</note>, Revati, and Hasta are favorable. So too are Punarvasu and Ashvini for the purchase and sale of animals. **Thus ends the section on the Buying and Selling of Cattle.**

Multiply the values of the <term>Sankranti: the passage of the Sun into a new zodiac sign</term>, the lunar day, and the day of the week together. Divide the total by three <note>original: "pāvaka," a word for fire which symbolically represents the number three</note> and look at the remainder. If the remainder is even, the sages say the market prices will be stable; if it is odd, prices will be high during that hour of the Sun. **Thus ends the section on Determining Prices.**

For pregnant women, take the number twenty <note>original: "nakha," meaning fingernails</note> and seven <note>original: "haya," meaning horse</note>, and calculate based on the current lunar day. Add one or subtract one, then divide by nine. If the remainder is an even number, it will be a girl <note>original: "kumārī"</note>; if it is odd, it will be a boy <note>original: "kumāra"</note>. **Thus ends the section on the Inquiry into the Birth of a Boy or Girl.**

Having first drawn the <term>Rashi Chakra: a zodiac wheel</term> starting with the Sun's entry into Aries <note>Mesha</note>, one should write the twenty-eight constellations within that circle. Place the Lion and the Horse in their respective positions. There are four "horns" <note>shringa</note> and eight "udders" <note>stana</note> positioned there. Wherever the constellation <term>Rohini</term> falls within this diagram, one may determine the good or bad fortune of the harvest. If it falls in the water, there will be much rain, which is very dear to <term>Indra: the god of rain and storms</term>. If in the ocean, it is auspicious but influenced by <term>Rahu: the shadow planet that causes eclipses</term>. On the mountain, there will be only a few drops of rain, and in the joints of the diagram, the rain will fall in the pits or depressions. **Thus ends the section on the Rohini Chakra.**

In the fixed signs of Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius <note>2, 5, 8, 11</note>... generated by the day of the week... in the dual-natured signs and Capricorn... in the signs of Aquarius, Pisces, Taurus, and Capricorn <note>11, 12, 2, 10</note>... victory in the circle... for the purpose of the mind... the Sun.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological timing (Muhurtas) found on page 11. It moves from general market and animal-related timings into the specific calculations for ritual fire placement, the movement of the deity Bhadra, and the foundational rules of planetary aspects and house significations.</meta>

<margin>12</margin>
# Page 12

[The calculation for the auspicious time] should be understood as full when the remainder is reached in the middle of the night. This is the **Consideration of the Auspicious Time**. <note>original: "puṇyakālavicāraḥ"</note> Subtract the lunar day and the day of the week, then divide by four. If the remainder is 3, 0, or 0 <note>This likely refers to specific results of the mathematical formula</note>, the **Residence of Fire** <term>Agnivāsa: the symbolic location of the ritual fire, determining if an oblation will be successful</term> is in the home for the sake of happiness. If the remainder is even, it results in the destruction of life and wealth.

In the heavens, on the earth, or in the underworld—wherever the Sun is positioned in the three signs, the Moon is positioned accordingly. The offerings to the fire are not recommended when the Moon is in certain positions, as the fire is considered "extinguished." **Thus ends the section on the Residence of Fire.**

In Pisces, Aries, Scorpio, and Cancer, <term>Bhadrā: a specific inauspicious period or the deity representing it</term> resides in **Heaven**.
In Virgo, Libra, Gemini, and Sagittarius, she resides in the **Underworld** <note>original: "nāgaloka," the world of the serpents</note>.
In Aquarius, Leo, Taurus, and Capricorn, she resides in the **World of Mortals** <note>original: "mṛtyuloka," the Earthly realm</note>.
Residence on Earth results in loss. **Thus ends the section on the Residence of Bhadra.**

The planets cast their gaze upon the third, ninth, fifth, fourth, eighth, and seventh houses with increasing strength of effect.
**Saturn** <note>original: "ravija," meaning 'son of the Sun'</note> looks with a full aspect at the third and tenth houses.
**Jupiter** <note>original: "jīva"</note> looks with a full aspect at the trines <note>the 5th and 9th houses</note>.
**Mars** <note>original: "bhūmisuta," meaning 'son of the Earth'</note> looks with a full aspect at the square houses <note>specifically the 4th and 8th</note>.
**Venus, the Sun, Mercury, and the Moon** <note>original: "sitārkabudhahimakara"</note> look with a full aspect at the seventh house <note>original: "kalatra," the house of the spouse</note>.
**Thus ends the section on the Aspects of the Planets.** <term>Dṛṣṭi: the 'glance' or aspect of a planet influencing other houses</term>

**Benefic Planets:** <note>original: "saumyagrahāḥ"</note> Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus.
**Malefic Planets:** <note>original: "krūragrahāḥ"</note> Sun, Mars, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu.

**The Houses from the Ascendant:** <term>Bhāva: the twelve astrological houses representing different life areas</term>
1. **Body** (Tanu)
2. **Wealth** (Dhana)
3. **Siblings** (Sahaja)
4. **Friends/Home** (Suhṛd)
5. **Children** (Suta)
6. **Enemies** (Ripu)
7. **Spouse** (Jāyā)
8. **Death/Longevity** (Mṛtyu)
9. **Dharma/Fortune** (Dharma)
10. **Career/Action** (Karma)
11. **Gains** (Āya)
12. **Loss/Expenditure** (Vyaya)

Whichever house is aspected by its own Lord or by benefic planets, that house will flourish and increase. If aspected by malefic planets, the loss of that house’s significations is indicated. Whether from the time of birth or the current transit, the combination with the Ascendant brings about such growth.

The Sun and Mars, located in the 8th, 10th, or 12th houses, act as the makers of wounds or difficulties. Saturn, the Moon, Jupiter, and Venus, when situated in the same sign as Jupiter, provide the answers for **Queries of the Hidden Fist**. <term>Muṣṭi-praśna: a branch of divination where the astrologer identifies an object held in the client's closed hand or a secret thought.</term>

When the Moon is in Aries, Taurus, Leo, or Capricorn, it is considered auspicious. In the moveable signs—Virgo and the rest—the planets are stripped of their enmity. Regarding the **Indication of the Lamp and Oil** <note>original: "dīpa-sneha-nirdeśaḥ," a technique using the metaphor of a lamp to describe the longevity of a situation or person</note>: the number of lamps corresponds to the rising sign's degree, and the amount of oil is told through the strength of the planets. If the Moon is seen in Aries at the time of birth, it indicates immediate danger or death.

When the planets are associated with auspicious signs and mixed influences, the result should be judged accordingly. One must understand the nature of the signs—whether male or female, and whether they are cruel or gentle—as well as the influence of a malefic planet in the eighth or twelfth houses. If such a planet is present, there is danger to the mother; similarly, a malefic in the tenth house brings danger to the father. **Thus ends the results of planetary combinations at the time of birth.** <note>original: "janmasamaye yogaphalam"</note>

The sequence of the year's results is determined from the day of birth and the ruling deities of the time. **Thus ends the determination of the year's results.** <note>original: "varṣaphala-nirṇaye"</note> Beginning with the constellation Krittika, one should count the group of stars and divide by nine. The remainder determines the **Planetary Period**. <term>Graha-daśā: a system of time-cycles in Indian astrology that predicts when specific karmic influences will manifest.</term>

The sequence for the planets and their durations is: Sun, Moon, Mars, Rahu, Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, Ketu, and Venus. The numerical values for these periods are:

Regarding the days of the period of Saturn: In the **House of the Body** <term>Tanu Sthana: The 1st house of a horoscope, representing the physical body and appearance</term> for a **female horoscope**, the planets produce various results. ...loses her children; but if the **Son of Bhrigu** <note>original: "Bhrigusute," referring to the planet Venus</note> is present, she is fertile and endowed with happiness. 

### 4. The House of Happiness
<note>Implicitly the 4th house, though the marker "4" appears after the description of Venus.</note>

### 5. <insert>House of Offspring</insert> 
<term>Suta-sthana: The 5th house, governing children and creativity.</term>
If the **Sun, Mercury, or Saturn** <note>original: "Ravi Jna Khala," where Khala refers to malefics, here specifically Saturn</note> are placed in the fifth house, she will have no sons. However, **Jupiter** and **Venus** give many sons. **Rahu** in this position brings death, while the **Sun and Mercury** together cause disease. The **Moon** <note>original: "Shashanka"</note> ensures the birth of daughters.

### 6. <insert>House of Enemies</insert>
<term>Ripu-sthana: The 6th house, relating to health, enemies, and debt.</term>
If **Saturn, the Sun, Rahu, or Mars** are stationed in the sixth house, she will have many daughters and share in great wealth. The **Moon** makes one a widow. **Mercury** <note>original: "Shashanka-tanaya," literally "Son of the Moon"</note> makes a woman beautiful but poor, or perhaps a prostitute and fond of quarreling. 

### 7. <insert>House of Marriage</insert>
<term>Jaya-sthana: The 7th house, governing the husband and partnerships.</term>
If **Saturn, Mars, Jupiter, Rahu, the Sun, Mercury, or Venus** are located in the seventh house, they bring (in order): death during childbirth, widowhood, imprisonment, fear, loss of wealth, disease, or death while traveling abroad. These results are certain.

### 8. <insert>House of Death</insert>
<term>Mrityu-sthana: The 8th house, governing longevity and secret things.</term>
If **Jupiter and Mercury** are in the eighth house, there is certain separation or death. The **Moon and Venus** <note>original: "Shashi Bhrigu-sutau"</note> produce the same. **Rahu and the Sun** make one a widow and poor. The **Son of the Sun** <note>original: "Suryatmaja," referring to Saturn</note> brings many diseases, yet she remains beloved by her husband.

### 9. <insert>House of Fortune</insert>
<term>Dharma-sthana: The 9th house, governing religion, luck, and father.</term>
If **Venus, the Sun, Mars, or Jupiter** are in the house of fortune, she is devoted to religious duties. **Mercury** makes her beautiful and fortunate. **Rahu** and **Saturn** make a woman barren. The **Moon** ensures she gives birth to sons.

### 10. <insert>House of Action</insert>
<term>Karma-sthana: The 10th house, governing career and public life.</term>
If **Rahu** is located in the sky-house <note>original: "Nabhastha," a synonym for the 10th house</note>, she becomes a widow. If **malefics, the Sun, or Saturn** are there, her children will die. **Mercury** <note>original: "Jna"</note> makes her devoid of wealth and like a sesame seed <note>possibly meaning insignificant or small-minded</note>. The **Moon** and the remaining planets make her wealthy and very much beloved.

### 11. <insert>House of Gains</insert>
<term>Aya-sthana: The 11th house, governing income and social circles.</term>
The **Sun** in the house of gains gives many daughters. The **Moon** makes her wealthy. **Venus and Mercury** <note>original: "Titisuto Ravijnau"</note> make her endowed with sons and very rich. **Jupiter and Venus** <note>original: "Suraguru Bhrigu-jah"</note> grant her a long life and daughters. **Rahu** makes her beautiful and modest.

### 12. <insert>House of Loss</insert>
<term>Vyaya-sthana: The 12th house, governing expenses and liberation.</term>
If planets are in this house, she spends her wealth. The **Sun** makes her poor. **Mercury** <note>original: "Jna"</note> makes her barren or devoted to other men...

A medicine for the teeth: Take Lodh <note>a tree bark used in Ayurveda for cooling and firming gums</note>, alum, black pepper, and mastic <note>original: "rumi sundak"</note>, eri, kircha, and tapki. ->**Decorative Marbled Cover**<-

This page consists of a piece of decorative hand-marbled paper, which likely served as either the front cover or the inner endpaper of the manuscript. 

<note>The design features a classic <term>nonpareil pattern</term> (original: "nonpareil," meaning "without equal"). This technique involves floating vibrant pigments—here, red, yellow, blue, and black—on a liquid surface and using a fine comb-like tool to create tight, uniform waves. This specific style of paper decoration was highly popular in bookbinding during the 18th and 19th centuries.</note>

This leaf does not contain any historical Sanskrit text or diagrams. It is an example of traditional decorative arts used to beautify and protect a bound volume.

<note>The title <term>Sārasangraha</term> translates to "a collection of the essence," indicating a work that synthesizes key points from larger treatises.
